  if (!window.__pdfModalInit) {
    window.__pdfModalInit = true;

    const canEmbed =
      typeof navigator.pdfViewerEnabled === "boolean"
        ? navigator.pdfViewerEnabled
        : !/Mobile|Android|iPhone|iPad/i.test(navigator.userAgent);

    const setupPdfModals = () => {
      document.querySelectorAll("[data-pdf-viewer]").forEach((viewer) => {
        const modalId = viewer.id.replace("-viewer", "");
        const overlay = document.getElementById(modalId);
        if (!overlay || overlay.__pdfBound) return;
        overlay.__pdfBound = true;

        if (overlay.parentElement !== document.body) {
          document.body.appendChild(overlay);
        }

        const obj = viewer.querySelector("object");
        const iframe = viewer.querySelector("iframe");
        const fallback = viewer.querySelector("[data-pdf-fallback]");

        if (!canEmbed) {
          if (obj) obj.style.display = "none";
          if (fallback) {
            fallback.classList.remove("hidden");
            fallback.classList.add("flex");
          }
          return;
        }

        const openHandler = () => {
          const src = viewer.dataset.pdfSrc;
          if (!src) return;
          if (obj) obj.setAttribute("data", src);
          if (iframe) iframe.setAttribute("src", src);
        };

        const closeHandler = () => {
          if (obj) obj.removeAttribute("data");
          if (iframe) iframe.removeAttribute("src");
        };

        overlay.addEventListener("open.hs.overlay", openHandler);
        overlay.addEventListener("close.hs.overlay", closeHandler);

        document.addEventListener(
          "astro:before-swap",
          () => {
            overlay.removeEventListener("open.hs.overlay", openHandler);
            overlay.removeEventListener("close.hs.overlay", closeHandler);
            overlay.__pdfBound = false;
          },
          { once: true },
        );
      });
    };

    document.addEventListener("astro:page-load", setupPdfModals);
    if (document.readyState !== "loading") setupPdfModals();
  }

class="grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-[1fr_340px] lg:gap-10" data-astro-cid-d5g7sy6c="true">  <div class="min-w-0 article-prose" id="article-content" data-astro-cid-d5g7sy6c="true"> <div class="prose prose-lg max-w-none text-void-950 prose-headings:font-heading prose-headings:text-void-950 prose-headings:font-bold prose-a:text-primary prose-strong:text-void-950 prose-blockquote:font-heading prose-blockquote:font-normal prose-blockquote:not-italic prose-blockquote:text-void-950 prose-img:rounded-lg"><p>AvosLocker is one of the newer <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/what-is/ransomware.html" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">ransomware</a> families that came to fill the void left by <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/ransomware-spotlight/ransomware-spotlight-revil" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">REvil</a>. While not as prominent or active as <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/ransomware-spotlight/ransomware-spotlight-lockbit" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">LockBit</a> or <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/ransomware-spotlight/ransomware-spotlight-conti" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Conti</a>, it is slowly making a name for itself, with the US Federal Bureau of Investigation (FBI) releasing an <a href="https://www.cisa.gov/uscert/ncas/current-activity/2022/03/22/fbi-and-fincen-release-advisory-avoslocker-ransomware" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">advisory</a> on this threat. According to the report, AvosLocker has been targeting critical infrastructure in different sectors of the US, with attacks also observed in other countries like Canada, UK, and Spain. Although detections are low, its clever use of familiar tactics makes it a ransomware variant worth monitoring today.</p><h2 id="what-do-organizations-need-to-know-about-avoslocker-k000u">What do organizations need to know about AvosLocker?</h2><p>AvosLocker is another variant that runs on a ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) model. It was first spotted in July 2021 and has since come up with several variants released over time. The following are the key characteristics of AvosLocker:</p><ul><li><strong>It uses the remote administration tool AnyDesk.</strong> One of the notable characteristics of AvosLocker campaigns is its use of AnyDesk, a remote administration tool (RAT) to connect to victim machines. Using this tool, the operator can manually operate and infect the machine.</li><li><strong>It runs on safe mode.</strong> Another key element of AvosLocker is running itself on safe mode as part of its evasion tactics. The attacker restarts the machine, disables certain drivers, and runs on safe mode, thus avoiding certain security measures that are unable to run in this mode. Operators also set up certain drivers to make sure that AnyDesk would run even in safe mode. It is important to note that this was a tactic previously employed by the now defunct REvil.</li><li><strong>Operators auction stolen data. </strong>AvosLocker again takes a leaf from REvilâs page by auctioning stolen data on its site, on top of its <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/cybercrime-and-digital-threats/ransomware-double-extortion-and-beyond-revil-clop-and-conti" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">double extortion</a> scheme. This could be the groupâs way of further monetizing a single successful attack or salvaging a failed one.</li></ul><p>As mentioned, AvosLocker operators have also released multiple versions of this ransomware. The tactic of running itself on safe mode was seen in the second version of AvosLocker. Following the trend of targeting Linux machines, AvosLocker also released a Linux variant as <a href="https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/linux-version-of-avoslocker-ransomware-targets-vmware-esxi-servers/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">advertised</a> by the group on October 2021. This variant is capable of attacking ESXi virtual machines (VMs), which makes it a variant to watch out for.</p><p>Operating as an RaaS, the actors behind AvosLocker conduct reconnaissance before each campaign. Actors choose their targets based on their ability to pay the demanded ransom and tailor their attacks accordingly.</p><p>In the next sections, we look at which regions and industries the group has targeted most often, based on our detections and information from their leak site.</p><h2 id="top-affected-industries-and-countries-k001m">Top affected industries and countries</h2><p>Our telemetry shows data on AvosLocker activity or attack attempts. While we observed AvosLocker activity from all over the world, India and Canada showed top detections from July 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022.</p><figure class="w-full my-6 clear-start"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/1179a209adaeed6aa08afe7d8952a39eab242fd4-1200x767.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="Countries with the highest number of attack attempts for AvosLocker ransomware (July 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022)" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="rounded-lg border border-gray-200 h-auto shadow-sm w-full" srcset="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/1179a209adaeed6aa08afe7d8952a39eab242fd4-1200x767.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1108w, https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/1179a209adaeed6aa08afe7d8952a39eab242fd4-1200x767.png?w=1200&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1200w" sizes="min(1108px, 100vw)"><figcaption class="mt-3 text-sm text-center text-gray-600"><span class="italic"> Figure 1. Countries with the highest number of attack attempts per machine for AvosLocker ransomware (July 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022) </span><span class="block mt-1 text-gray-500 [&amp;_a]:no-underline [&amp;_a]:hover:underline [&amp;_a]:text-trendred-500"> <p>Source:Â Trend Microâ¢ Smart Protection Networkâ¢</p> </span></figcaption></figure><p>Based on our detections, AvosLocker was the most active in the food and beverage sector, followed by the technology and finance sectors. However, there is only by a slim margin given the small sample size.Â </p><figure class="w-full my-6 clear-start"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/cca76d3c61e19a5b8253a94308f468941385365b-1200x686.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="Based on our detections, AvosLocker was the most active in the food and beverage sector, followed by the technology and finance sectors. However, there is only by a slim margin given the small sample size." loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="rounded-lg border border-gray-200 h-auto shadow-sm w-full" srcset="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/cca76d3c61e19a5b8253a94308f468941385365b-1200x686.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1108w, https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/cca76d3c61e19a5b8253a94308f468941385365b-1200x686.png?w=1200&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1200w" sizes="min(1108px, 100vw)"><figcaption class="mt-3 text-sm text-center text-gray-600"><span class="italic"> Figure 2. Based on our detections, AvosLocker was the most active in the food and beverage sector, followed by the technology and finance sectors. However, there is only a slim margin given the small sample size. </span><span class="block mt-1 text-gray-500 [&amp;_a]:no-underline [&amp;_a]:hover:underline [&amp;_a]:text-trendred-500"> <p>Source: Trend Micro Smart Protection Network</p> </span></figcaption></figure><p>As of this writing, the highest number of AvosLocker-related detections we have seen was in the month of February, which continues the sudden increase observed at the start of the year.</p><figure class="w-full my-6 clear-start"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/be16ab9b5d9030b2c3d9d1cc45a7c212cc7ed42a-1200x446.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="AvosLocker monthly detections per machine (July 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022)" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="rounded-lg border border-gray-200 h-auto shadow-sm w-full" srcset="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/be16ab9b5d9030b2c3d9d1cc45a7c212cc7ed42a-1200x446.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1108w, https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/be16ab9b5d9030b2c3d9d1cc45a7c212cc7ed42a-1200x446.png?w=1200&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1200w" sizes="min(1108px, 100vw)"><figcaption class="mt-3 text-sm text-center text-gray-600"><span class="italic"> Figure 3. AvosLocker monthly detections per machine (July 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022) </span><span class="block mt-1 text-gray-500 [&amp;_a]:no-underline [&amp;_a]:hover:underline [&amp;_a]:text-trendred-500"> <p>Source: Trend Micro Smart Protection Network</p> </span></figcaption></figure><h2 id="targeted-regions-and-sectors-according-to-avoslocker-leak-site-k001x">Targeted regions and sectors according to AvosLocker leak site</h2><p>We also ventured into AvosLockerâs leak site, which offered a different perspective on its targets. From December 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022 we found 15 listed entities. The organizations listed in the site were successfully attacked and have not, in that period, paid the demanded ransom.</p><p>By grouping the list according to regions, we found that AvosLocker focused its efforts on targets from North America.</p><figure class="w-full my-6 clear-start"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/0edea998fd50c0c8fb95ae12b2e204b4497a8ab2-1200x327.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="Regional distribution of AvosLocker victims according to the groupâs leak site (December 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022)" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="rounded-lg border border-gray-200 h-auto shadow-sm w-full" srcset="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/0edea998fd50c0c8fb95ae12b2e204b4497a8ab2-1200x327.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1108w, https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/0edea998fd50c0c8fb95ae12b2e204b4497a8ab2-1200x327.png?w=1200&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1200w" sizes="min(1108px, 100vw)"><figcaption class="mt-3 text-sm text-center text-gray-600"><span class="italic"> Figure 4. Regional distribution of AvosLocker victims according to the groupâs leak site (December 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022) </span></figcaption></figure><p>More than half of the 15 entities we found in the leak site were small enterprises. With respect to the targetsâ specific industries, we saw no trend emerging, as no one industry stood out from the others.Â  This can be seen in Figure 6, where no single industry stood out from the rest.</p><figure class="w-full my-6 clear-start"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/f3ee1b9f85ff5b258e1e67b573c3bfccfbc5e90b-1200x742.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="Sector distribution of AvosLocker victims according to the groupâs leak site (December 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022)" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="rounded-lg border border-gray-200 h-auto shadow-sm w-full" srcset="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/f3ee1b9f85ff5b258e1e67b573c3bfccfbc5e90b-1200x742.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1108w, https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/f3ee1b9f85ff5b258e1e67b573c3bfccfbc5e90b-1200x742.png?w=1200&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1200w" sizes="min(1108px, 100vw)"><figcaption class="mt-3 text-sm text-center text-gray-600"><span class="italic"> Figure 5. Sector distribution of AvosLocker victims according to the groupâs leak site (December 1, 2021 to February 28, 2022) </span></figcaption></figure><p>We do note, however, that AvosLocker has showed relatively less activity compared to other more prominent ransomware families in terms of our detections and observations from its leak site. Because of the limited sample size, further monitoring might be necessary to identify trends.</p><h2 id="infection-chain-and-techniques-k0029">Infection chain and techniques</h2><p>The AvosLocker infection chain, which operates on the RaaS model, can vary depending on the target. The following infection chain shows a variety of tactics and tools employed by this RaaS.</p><figure class="w-full my-6 clear-start"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/b0b3be6835e19bdb68de55c7c9749bbfea24d01f-1500x999.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="AvosLocker infection chain" loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="rounded-lg border border-gray-200 h-auto shadow-sm w-full" srcset="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/b0b3be6835e19bdb68de55c7c9749bbfea24d01f-1500x999.png?w=1108&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1108w, https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/b0b3be6835e19bdb68de55c7c9749bbfea24d01f-1500x999.png?w=1500&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format 1500w" sizes="min(1108px, 100vw)"><figcaption class="mt-3 text-sm text-center text-gray-600"><span class="italic"> Figure 6. AvosLocker infection chain </span></figcaption></figure><div class="tactic-stack" data-full-bleed><div class="tactic-row"><div class="tactic-row-inner"><div class="tactic-col"><div class="tactic-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/initial-access.png" alt="Initial Access" class="tactic-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="tactic-body"><h3 class="tactic-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground tactic-heading"> Initial Access </h3><ul class="tactic-list"><li><p>AvosLocker uses Zoho ManageEngine ServiceDesk Plus and its exploit for initial access and to download of web shell and AnyDesk.</p></li><li><p>It has been reported to make use of compromised accounts to access its victims via RDP or virtual private network (VPN).</p></li></ul></div></div><div class="tactic-col"><div class="tactic-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/defense-evasion.png" alt="Defense Evasion, Discovery, and Credential Access" class="tactic-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="tactic-body"><h3 class="tactic-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground tactic-heading"> Defense Evasion, Discovery, and Credential Access </h3><ul class="tactic-list"><li><p>It uses Avast Anti-Rootkit Driver and a PowerShell script to disable certain antivirus processes.</p></li><li><p>It uses a BAT script to disable antivirus services that can run on Windows Safe Mode.</p></li><li><p>It uses Mimikatz and XenArmor Password Recovery Pro Tool to get credentials.</p></li><li><p>It also uses Nmap, NetScan, and native Windows commands (such as ipconfig, nslookup, and others) to perform discovery on the target network.</p></li><li><p>It avoids writing the ransomware payload in target systems.</p></li></ul></div></div></div></div><div class="tactic-row"><div class="tactic-row-inner"><div class="tactic-col"><div class="tactic-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/discovery.png" alt="Lateral Movement and Command and Control" class="tactic-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="tactic-body"><h3 class="tactic-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground tactic-heading"> Lateral Movement and Command and Control </h3><ul class="tactic-list"><li><p>AvosLocker installs AnyDesk to gain control of the targeted systems.</p></li><li><p>It uses PDQ Deploy to push out and execute the Windows batch script on the targeted systems.</p></li></ul></div></div><div class="tactic-col"><div class="tactic-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/lateral-movement.png" alt="Impact" class="tactic-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="tactic-body"><h3 class="tactic-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground tactic-heading"> Impact </h3><ul class="tactic-list"><li><p>It then executes the ransomware payload (AvosLocker) to perform its encryption routine once all other routines are done.</p></li><li><p>It now has both Windows and Linux version of this ransomware payload. The Linux version is also known to terminate ESXi virtual machines.</p></li><li><p>In its latest attacks, the Windows version was executed after restarting in safe mode to inhibit security software from detecting the ransomware variant.</p></li><li><p>In order to execute on safe mode, it adds a RunOnce registry entry under autostart. Further investigation revealed multiple ways AvosLocker can be executed via the RunOnce registry, which are the following:</p><ul class="tactic-sublist"><li><p>Direct execution of the ransomware payload</p></li><li><p>Execute a PowerShell script that will download and execute the ransomware payload</p></li><li><p>Execute a PowerShell script that will decode and execute the ransomware payload from a disguised .jpg file.</p></li></ul></li><li><p>It drops a ransom note similar to the one in Figure 7.</p><figure class="tactic-item-figure"><img src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/ch6z6vqj/production/439a5dd3535ecf74d2efc19d99a3b48b37256561-694x294.png?w=694&amp;fit=max&amp;auto=format" alt="Sample ransom note used by AvosLocker" loading="lazy" decoding="async"><figcaption class="tactic-item-caption"><span>Figure 7. Sample ransom note used by AvosLocker</span></figcaption></figure></li></ul></div></div></div></div><div class="tactic-row"><div class="tactic-row-inner"><div class="tactic-col"><div class="tactic-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/execution.png" alt="Other technical details" class="tactic-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="tactic-body"><h3 class="tactic-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground tactic-heading"> Other technical details </h3><ul class="tactic-list"><li><p>It avoids the following directories:</p></li><li><p>All Users</p></li><li><p>AppData</p></li><li><p>boot</p></li><li><p>bootmgr</p></li><li><p>Games</p></li><li><p>Intel</p></li><li><p>Microsoft. (Directory name starts with âMicrosoft.)</p></li><li><p>Program Files</p></li><li><p>ProgramData</p></li><li><p>Public</p></li><li><p>Sophos</p></li><li><p>System Volume Information</p></li><li><p>Windows</p></li><li><p>Windows.old</p></li><li><p>WinNT</p></li><li><p>It avoids encrypting the following files with strings in their file name:</p></li><li><p>autorun.inf</p></li><li><p>boot.ini</p></li><li><p>bootfont.bin</p></li><li><p>bootsect.bak</p></li><li><p>config.msi</p></li><li><p>desktop.ini</p></li><li><p>iconcache.db</p></li><li><p>ntldr</p></li><li><p>ntuser.dat</p></li><li><p>ntuser.dat.log</p></li><li><p>ntuser.ini</p></li><li><p>thumbs.db</p></li><li><p>Thumbs.db</p></li><li><p>It avoids encrypting files with the following extensions:</p></li><li><p>.386</p></li><li><p>.adv</p></li><li><p>.ani</p></li><li><p>.avos</p></li><li><p>.avos2</p></li><li><p>.avos2j</p></li><li><p>.avoslinux</p></li><li><p>.bat</p></li><li><p>.bin</p></li><li><p>.cab</p></li><li><p>.cmd</p></li><li><p>.com</p></li><li><p>.cpl</p></li><li><p>.cur</p></li><li><p>.deskthemepack</p></li><li><p>.diagcab</p></li><li><p>.diagcfg</p></li><li><p>.diagpkg</p></li><li><p>.dll</p></li><li><p>.drv</p></li><li><p>.exe</p></li><li><p>.hlp</p></li><li><p>.hta</p></li><li><p>.icl</p></li><li><p>.icns</p></li><li><p>.ico</p></li><li><p>.ics</p></li><li><p>.idx</p></li><li><p>.key</p></li><li><p>.ldf</p></li><li><p>.lnk</p></li><li><p>.lock</p></li><li><p>.mod</p></li><li><p>.mpa</p></li><li><p>.msc</p></li><li><p>.msi</p></li><li><p>.msp</p></li><li><p>.msstyles</p></li><li><p>.msu</p></li><li><p>.nls</p></li><li><p>.nomedia</p></li><li><p>.ocx</p></li><li><p>.pdb</p></li><li><p>.prf</p></li><li><p>.ps1</p></li><li><p>.rom</p></li><li><p>.rtp</p></li><li><p>.scr</p></li><li><p>.shs</p></li><li><p>.spl</p></li><li><p>.sys</p></li><li><p>.theme</p></li><li><p>.themepack</p></li><li><p>.wpx</p></li><li><p>It terminates the following processes:</p></li><li><p>encsvc</p></li><li><p>thebat</p></li><li><p>mydesktopq os</p></li><li><p>xfssvccon</p></li><li><p>firefox</p></li><li><p>infopath</p></li><li><p>winword</p></li><li><p>steam</p></li><li><p>synctime</p></li><li><p>notepad</p></li><li><p>ocomm</p></li><li><p>onenote</p></li><li><p>mspub</p></li><li><p>thunderbird</p></li><li><p>agntsvc</p></li><li><p>sql</p></li><li><p>excel</p></li><li><p>powerpnt</p></li><li><p>outlook</p></li><li><p>wordpad</p></li><li><p>dbeng50</p></li><li><p>isqlplussvc</p></li></ul></div></div></div></div></div><h2 id="mitre-tactics-and-techniques-k00d3">MITRE tactics and techniques</h2><figure class="mitre-grid-wrap my-8" data-full-bleed><div class="mitre-grid-scroll"><div class="mitre-grid" style="grid-template-columns: repeat(9, minmax(14rem, 1fr));"><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Initial Access</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1190</strong> - Exploit public-facing application<br><em><em>Arrives by exploiting Zoho ManageEngine ServiceDesk Plus Exploit to download web shell and AnyDesk <br><br>As it operates as a RaaS, depending on the affiliate, the following exploits might be used for initial access:</em><em>â¢<span> </span><span>CVE-2021-31206</span></em><em>â¢&#160;<span>CVE-2021-31207</span></em><em>â¢&#160;<span>CVE-2021-34473</span></em><em>â¢&#160;<span>CVE-2021-34523</span></em><em>â¢&#160;<span>CVE-2021-26855</span></em></em></p><p><strong>T1078</strong> - Valid accounts<br><em>Have been reported to make used of compromised accounts to access victims via RDP or VPN</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Execution</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1059</strong>&#160;- Command and scripting interpreter<br><em>Uses various scripting interpreters like PowerShell and Windows Command shell</em></p><p><strong>T1072</strong>&#160;-&#160;Software deployment tools<br><em>Used PDQ Deploy to distribute the batch file and payload on target computers</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Persistence</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1136</strong> - Create account<br> <em>Creates a new user to ensure automatic login when machine is restarted in safe mode</em></p><p><strong>T1547</strong> -&#160;Boot or logon autostart execution<br><em>Creates an autostart entry to ensure execution of ransomware when restarted in safe mode</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Defense Evasion</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1112</strong>&#160;-&#160;Modify registry<br><em><em>Modifies registry entry to allow AnyDesk on safe mode and to enable automatic login when restarted in safe mode</em></em></p><p><strong>T1562</strong>&#160;-&#160;Impair defenses<br><em>Abuses Avast Anti-Rootkit Driver and a PowerShell script to disable certain processes related to security tools and also restarts the machine in safe mode to inhibit security tools from executing </em></p><p><strong>T1140</strong>&#160;-&#160;Deobfuscate/Decode files or information<br><em>Some ransomware samples are decoded using CertUtil and strings to be used by the ransomware are encrypted using XOR. </em></p><p><strong>T1070</strong>&#160;-&#160;Indicator removal on host<br><em>It deletes created registry entries, scripts, and ransomware binary after encryption.<br></em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Credential Access</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1003</strong> - OS credential dumping<br><em>Might utilize Mimikatz to dump credentials</em></p><p><strong>T1552</strong> -&#160;Unsecured credentials<br><em>Might utilize Mimikatz or XenArmor Password Recovery Pro tool to gather credentials</em></p><p><strong>T1555</strong> -&#160;Credentials from password stores<br><em>Might utilize XenArmor Password Recovery Pro tool to gain credentials</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Discovery</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1083</strong> - File and directory discovery<br><em>Searches for specific files and directory related to its ransomware encryption<br></em></p><p><strong>T1135</strong> - Network share discovery<br><em>Makes use of tools to enumerate network share</em></p><p><strong>T1057</strong> -&#160;Process discovery<br><em>Discovers certain processes for process termination</em></p><p><strong>T1018</strong> -&#160;Remote system discovery<br><em>Makes use of tools for network scans</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Lateral Movement</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1021</strong>&#160;- Remote services<br><em>Might use AnyDesk to remotely connect and transfer files</em></p><p><strong>T1072</strong> - Software deployment tools <em>Used PDQ Deploy to distribute the batch file and payload on target computers</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Command and Control</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1219</strong>&#160;- Remote access software<br><em>Makes use of tools for <br>network scans</em></p></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Impact</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><p><strong>T1486</strong>&#160;- Data encrypted for impact<br><em>Might use AnyDesk to remotely connect and transfer files</em><br> <em>It uses the salsa12 stream cipher to encrypt victim files. Older versions used advanced encryption standard (AES) AES-256-CBC and RSA to encrypt files and keys respectively.</em></p><p><strong>T1489</strong> -&#160;Service stop<br><em>Contains a list of services to be terminated to ensure encryption</em></p><p><strong>T1490</strong> -&#160;Inhibit system recovery<br><em>Deletes shadow copies</em></p><p><strong>T1491</strong> -&#160;Defacement<br><em>Replaces the desktop wallpaper with the ransom note</em></p></div></div></div></div></figure><h2 id="summary-of-malware-tools-and-exploits-used-k00d6">Summary of malware, tools, and exploits used</h2><p>Security teams can watch out for the presence of the following malware tools and exploits that are typically used in AvosLocker attacks:</p><figure class="mitre-grid-wrap my-8" data-full-bleed><div class="mitre-grid-scroll"><div class="mitre-grid" style="grid-template-columns: repeat(7, minmax(14rem, 1fr));"><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Initial Access</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>Exploit for Zoho ManageEngine ServiceDesk Plus</li></ul></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Execution</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>PowerShell</li><li>Windows command shell<strong></strong></li></ul></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Credential Access</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>Mimikatz</li><li>XenArmor Password Recovery Tool Pro</li></ul></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Discovery</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>NetScan</li><li>Nmap</li></ul></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Lateral Movement</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>PDQ Deploy</li></ul></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Defense Evasion</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>BAT file</li><li>Avast Anti-Rootkit Scanner</li><li>PowerShell script</li></ul></div></div><div class="mitre-col"><div class="mitre-col-header">Command and Control</div><div class="mitre-col-body"><ul><li>AnyDesk</li></ul></div></div></div></div></figure><h2 id="recommendations-k00db">Recommendations</h2><p>While AvosLocker is not yet as prominent as other ransomware families like <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/ransomware-spotlight/ransomware-spotlight-lockbit" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">LockBit</a>, <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/ransomware-spotlight/ransomware-spotlight-conti" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Conti</a>, and <a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/vinfo/us/security/news/ransomware-spotlight/ransomware-spotlight-clop" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Clop</a>, it seems to follow in the footsteps of these more established players. It also reuses tactics that worked for infamous ransomware families, namely REvil. This should be enough reason for organizations to keep an eye on this ransomware family as well as to stay abreast with the latest trends and tactics employed by threat actors today.</p><p>To help defend systems against similar threats, organizations can establish security frameworks that can allocate resources systematically for establishing solid defenses against ransomware.</p><p>Here are some best practices that can be included in these frameworks:</p><div class="rec-grid-wrap"><div class="rec-grid-inner"><div class="rec-grid"><div class="rec-card"><div class="rec-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/audit-inventory.png" alt="Audit and inventory" class="rec-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="rec-card-body"><h3 class="rec-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground rec-heading"> Audit and inventory </h3><span class="rec-heading-accent"></span><ul class="rec-list"><li>Take an inventory of assets and data.</li><li>Identify authorized and unauthorized devices and software.</li><li>Make an audit of event and incident logs.</li></ul></div></div><div class="rec-card"><div class="rec-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/command-and-control.png" alt="Configure and monitor" class="rec-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="rec-card-body"><h3 class="rec-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground rec-heading"> Configure and monitor </h3><span class="rec-heading-accent"></span><ul class="rec-list"><li>Manage hardware and software configurations.</li><li>Grant admin privileges and access only when necessary to an employeeâs role.</li><li>Monitor network ports, protocols, and services.</li><li>Activate security configurations on network infrastructure devices such as firewalls and routers.</li><li>Establish a software allowlist that only executes legitimate applications.</li></ul></div></div><div class="rec-card"><div class="rec-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/patch-update.png" alt="Patch and update" class="rec-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="rec-card-body"><h3 class="rec-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground rec-heading"> Patch and update </h3><span class="rec-heading-accent"></span><ul class="rec-list"><li>Conduct regular vulnerability assessments.</li><li>Perform patching or virtual patching for operating systems and applications.</li><li>Update software and applications to their latest versions.</li></ul></div></div><div class="rec-card"><div class="rec-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/protect-recover.png" alt="Protect and recover" class="rec-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="rec-card-body"><h3 class="rec-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground rec-heading"> Protect and recover </h3><span class="rec-heading-accent"></span><ul class="rec-list"><li>Implement data protection, back up, and recovery measures.</li><li>Enable multifactor authentication (MFA).</li></ul></div></div><div class="rec-card"><div class="rec-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/secure-defend.png" alt="Secure and defend" class="rec-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="rec-card-body"><h3 class="rec-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground rec-heading"> Secure and defend </h3><span class="rec-heading-accent"></span><ul class="rec-list"><li>Employ sandbox analysis to block malicious emails.</li><li>Deploy the latest versions of security solutions to all layers of the system, including email, endpoint, web, and network.</li><li>Detect early signs of an attack such as the presence of suspicious tools in the system.</li><li>Use advanced detection technologies such as those powered by AI and machine learning.</li></ul></div></div><div class="rec-card"><div class="rec-icon-wrap"><img src="/images/mitre-icons/train-test.png" alt="Train and test" class="rec-icon" loading="lazy"></div><div class="rec-card-body"><h3 class="rec-heading font-heading font-bold text-foreground rec-heading"> Train and test </h3><span class="rec-heading-accent"></span><ul class="rec-list"><li>Regularly train and assess employees on security skills.</li><li>Conduct red-team exercises and penetration tests.</li></ul></div></div></div></div></div><p>A multilayered approach can help organizations guard possible entry points into the system (endpoint, email, web, and network). Security solutions that can detect malicious components and suspicious behavior can also help protect enterprises.</p><ul><li><a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/business/products/detection-response.html" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Trend Micro Vision Oneâ¢</a>Â provides multilayered protection and behavior detection, which helps block questionable behavior and tools before the ransomware can do any damage.</li><li><a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/business/products/hybrid-cloud/cloud-one-workload-security.html" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Trend Micro Cloud Oneâ¢ Workload Security</a>Â protects systems against both known and unknown threats that exploit vulnerabilities. This protection is made possible through techniques such as virtual patching and machine learning.</li><li><a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/business/products/user-protection/sps/email-and-collaboration/email-inspector.html" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Trend Microâ¢ Deep Discoveryâ¢ Email Inspector</a>Â employs custom sandboxing and advanced analysis techniques to effectively block malicious emails, including phishing emails that can serve as entry points for ransomware.</li><li><a href="https://www.trendmicro.com/en_us/business/products/user-protection/sps/endpoint.html" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">Trend Micro Apex Oneâ¢</a>Â offers next-level automated threat detection and response against advanced concerns such as fileless threats and ransomware, ensuring the protection of endpoints.</li></ul><h2 id="indicators-of-compromise-iocs-k00eh">Indicators of Compromise (IOCs)</h2><p>The IOCs for this article can be found <a href="https://cdn.sanity.io/media-libraries/mllBRzE9QpZ8/files/containers/3GxASHbzE1gr148YKLxhqJFx92l/ioc-avoslocker-spotlightrsjk7pc.txt" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="underline decoration-1 decoration-primary/80 underline-offset-2 transition-[text-decoration-thickness] hover:decoration-2 hover:decoration-primary">here</a>. Actual indicators might vary per attack.</p></div>  </div> <aside class="hidden lg:block" data-astro-cid-d5g7sy6c> <div class="article-sidebar mb-[var(--spacing-24)]" data-astro-cid-d5g7sy6c> <div       var __isTouchDevice = window.matchMedia("(pointer: coarse)").matches;
      function __disableCarouselDragOnTouch() {
        if (!__isTouchDevice) return;
        document.querySelectorAll("[data-hs-carousel]").forEach(function (el) {
          try {
            var config = JSON.parse(el.getAttribute("data-hs-carousel"));
            if (config.isDraggable) {
              config.isDraggable = false;
              el.setAttribute("data-hs-carousel", JSON.stringify(config));

          }
        });
      }
